Q1. After a delay, 80 of 180 records are completed by 8 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 10 days?
A. 8 workers
B. 2 workers
C. 1.6 workers
D. 4 workers
Answer: 2 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{100}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 2 workers

---

Q2. A pipe transfers 15 litres of water every minute. How many litres are transferred in 30 minutes?
A. 446 litres
B. 450 litres
C. 449 litres
D. 540 litres
Answer: 450 litres
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_capacity_leakage_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=15\times 30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=450
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 450 litres

---

Q3. A group is expected to complete a work in 18 days. It has already worked for 8 days. What fraction remains?
A. 9/5
B. 4/9
C. 5/9
D. 5/4
Answer: 5/9
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{18}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{10}{18}=5/9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{18-8}{18}=5/9
\]
Answer: 5/9

---

Q4. A team has 2 men and 3 women with 4 children. They finish 4, 2, 1 boxes per person per day respectively. How many days are needed for 300 boxes?
A. 18 2/3 days
B. 13 1/3 days
C. 22 2/3 days
D. 16 2/3 days
Answer: 16 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women_children
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{300}{2\times 4+3\times 2+4\times 1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=16\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 16 2/3 days

---

Q5. A group is expected to complete a work in 20 days. It has already worked for 8 days. What fraction remains?
A. 3/2
B. 3/5
C. 2/5
D. 5/3
Answer: 3/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{20}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{12}{20}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{20-8}{20}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

---

Q6. For 4 days a team completes 133 forms per day. Then the team changes and completes 88 forms per day. How many days will 840 forms take altogether?
A. 9 days
B. 9 1/2 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 3 1/2 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=133\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{308}{88}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

---

Q7. An examiner checks 36 answer sheets in 4 days. How many answer sheets are checked per day?
A. 6.75 answer sheets
B. 11 answer sheets
C. 9 answer sheets
D. 14 answer sheets
Answer: 9 answer sheets

Explanation:

\[
r=\frac{W}{t}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r=\frac{36}{4}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
r=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
r=W/t
\]
Answer: 9 answer sheets

Q8. A contractor earns ₹9000 for a job and gets ₹150 per day as bonus for finishing early. If the work is 3 days early, what is the earning?
A. ₹9446
B. ₹9450
C. ₹9448
D. ₹14175
Answer: ₹9450
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_contract_penalty_bonus
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
E=base+bonus
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
E=9000+3\times 150
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
E=9450
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=base+adjustment
\]
Answer: ₹9450

---

Q9. If \(\frac{1}{3}\) of a piece of work takes 6 days, in how many days will the entire work be completed?
A. 23 days
B. 2 days
C. 9 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=6\times \frac{3}{1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=6\div \frac{1}{3}=18
\]
Answer: 18 days

Q10. A packing job has 24 boxes. The team packs 3 boxes daily at first for 5 days, and then 9 boxes daily. How many days are needed in all?
A. 7 days
B. 7 1/2 days
C. 12 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{9}{9}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6 days

---

Q11. Food is sufficient for 20 people for 36 days. After 5 days, the number of people becomes 25. For how many more days will the food last?
A. 25 4/5 days
B. 37 1/5 days
C. 24 4/5 days
D. 20 4/5 days
Answer: 24 4/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-20\times 5}{25}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=24.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 24 4/5 days

---

Q12. Two filling pipes A and B take 12 and 18 hours respectively. If Pipe A is closed after 2 hours, how many hours are needed to fill the tank?
A. 15 hours
B. 11 hours
C. 18 hours
D. 14 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

---

Q13. A, B and C work on successive days in that order. They pack 8, 5 and 3 boxes respectively on their turns. If 120 boxes are to be packed, in how many days will the job be completed?
A. 25 days
B. 27 1/2 days
C. 22 days
D. 28 days
Answer: 22 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+5+3
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=22
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 22 days

---

Q14. A with B takes 10 days, B with C takes 12 days and C with A takes 15 days. How many days will A alone take?
A. 24 days
B. 28 days
C. 22 days
D. 19 1/5 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q15. Machines A, B and C operate for 5 hours and make 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour. How many parts are produced?
A. 281.25 items
B. 225 items
C. 220 items
D. 224 items
Answer: 225 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_parallel_machine_batches
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 5+15\times 5+18\times 5
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=225
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 225 items

---

Q16. A can complete a piece of work in 12 days and B can complete the same work in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 36 days
B. 12 days
C. 8 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/12=2,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{2+1}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 24}{12+24}=8
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

x=\frac{120-60}{6}
\]

x=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:

Q18. A job earns ₹12000 for the workers. If their work shares are in the ratio 30:30:12, what is C's wage?
A. ₹2003
B. ₹2000
C. ₹2006
D. ₹2500
Answer: ₹2000
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 12000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=2000
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹2000

Q19. A needs 15 days for a work and A with B needs 12 days. In how many days can B do the work alone?
A. 60 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 3 days
D. 27 days
Answer: 60 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
B=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 15}{15-12}=60
\]
Answer: 60 days

Q20. A worker makes 15 toys per day for the first 5 days. After that, the worker makes 12 toys per day. How many days are needed to make 180 toys?

\[
T=5+\frac{180-15\times 5}{12}

\]

\[

Q21. A and B can separately complete a job in 8 days and 24 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 6 days
B. 8 days
C. 32 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+1}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 24}{8+24}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q22. A filling pipe and a drain work alternately for one hour each. The pipe adds 12 litres and the drain removes 5 litres. How many hours are needed for a 60-litre tank?
A. 21 1/3 hours
B. 17 1/3 hours
C. 19 3/5 hours
D. 16 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

---

Q25. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. After 2 hours, a leak begins which empties a full tank in 30 hours. Find the total time to fill the tank?

D. 23 2/3 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

\[

\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

Q27. A ration stock is enough for 30 people for 30 days. Find the number of days for 40 people?

\[

30\times 30=40\times D_2

Q28. A and B can separately complete a work in 10 and 20 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 12 days
B. 9 days
C. 13 1/2 days
D. 5 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
18(T-2)+9(T-3)=180
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

---

Q33. Pipe A is an inlet of 12 hours and pipe B is an outlet of 36 hours. If both are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 12 hours
B. 14 hours
C. 15 hours
D. 10 hours
Answer: 10 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 10 hours

\]

Q38. 18 painters can paint a building in 8 days by working 5 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 13 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 27 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q39. A team requires 6 working days for a job. If work is interrupted for 1 day, what is the total duration?
A. 7 days
B. 5 days
C. 3 days
D. 10 1/2 days
Answer: 7 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{66}{7+4}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+1=7
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 7 days

\]

Q45. A tank has \(\frac{1}{4}\) of its capacity filled. A pipe fills the complete tank in 12 hours. What is the time needed now?

\[

\]

\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]

\[
T=9
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 9 hours

\]
Answer: 12 days

Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=480
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]

Q82. A tank is filled by a pipe in 15 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 25 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?

B. 43 1/2 hours
C. 37 1/2 hours
D. 39 1/2 hours
Answer: 37 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:

\[

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{25}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=37.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 37 1/2 hours

Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 9000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1500
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1500

Q114. A and B together finish a job in 10 days. A alone takes 12 days. How many days will B alone take?

D. 22 days

\[

\[

\]

\]

Q116. A can complete a piece of work in 15 days and B can complete the same work in 12 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 27 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 12 days
D. 6 2/3 days
Answer: 6 2/3 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(15,12)=60
\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+5}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 12}{15+12}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

Q121. In a joint job, A alone needs 12 days and B alone needs 10 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?

C. 10 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
20(T-2)+24(T-3)=240
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q123. Only alternate days are working days for A. A finishes 10 metres each working day. How many calendar days are required for 60 metres?
A. 11 days
B. 16 days
C. 12 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_work_rest_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+0
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

\]

\]

\]

Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 hours

Q155. 15 workers finish a job in 16 days, working 6 hours daily. How many workers should work 8 hours daily to finish it in 12 days?

D. 15 workers

\[

\[

\]

\[
M_2=15
\]

\]
Answer: 15 workers

\]

Q225. One pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, while another empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled with both open?
A. 21 hours
B. 26 hours
C. 20 hours
D. 25 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

---

Q226. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A prints 10 pages per turn and B prints 4 pages per turn. In how many hours will 120 pages be printed?

Answer: 16 4/5 hours

Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=16.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 16 4/5 hours

Q227. A machine prints 80 pages per hour. It runs for 8 hours and 95% pages are usable. How many usable pages are obtained?
A. 608 pages
B. 603 pages
C. 605 pages
D. 760 pages
Answer: 608 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_work_quality_rejection
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
O_a=O\times \frac{p}{100}
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
O_a=80\times 8\times \frac{95}{100}
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
O_a=608
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O_a=gross\times p/100
\]
Answer: 608 pages

Q228. The efficiency ratio A:B is 4:7, while B:C is 7:8. Find the ratio A:B:C?
A. 8:7:4
B. 4:7:8:2
C. 4:7:8:3
D. 4:7:8
Answer: 4:7:8
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:

\[
B_c
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
LCM(7,7)=7
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Answer: 4:7:8

Q229. A work requires 12 workers for 10 days with 8 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 16 days with 6 hours per day?
A. 12 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 19 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q230. A job needs 15 days of work. Work stops for 2 days during the job. How many days pass from start to finish?
A. 19 days
B. 17 days
C. 25 1/2 days
D. 21 days
Answer: 17 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{240}{12+4}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=17
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 17 days

Q233. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 12 days and B alone needs 18 days. A leaves after 4 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?
A. 14 2/5 days
B. 8 days
C. 12 days
D. 14 days
Answer: 12 days

Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:

\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(6+4)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{32}{4}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q235. A alone takes 8 days and B alone takes 12 days. A works alone for 4 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?

\]
Answer: 6 2/5 days

\]

Q299. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 30 days and B alone needs 18 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?

C. 18 4/5 days
D. 16 4/5 days
Answer: 16 4/5 days

Explanation:

\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+5)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{74}{5}=16.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 16 4/5 days

Q301. A starts a work and B joins after 2 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 20 and 12 days. What is the total time taken?

D. 4 3/4 days
Answer: 8 3/4 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join

\[

\]

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[

\]

T=2+\frac{180-9\times 2}{9+15}
\]
Answer: 8 3/4 days

Q305. A tank is filled by a pipe in 12 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 18 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 31 hours
B. 45 hours
C. 36 hours
D. 35 hours
Answer: 36 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 36 hours

Q331. A alone finishes a job in 10 days, while B alone finishes it in 30 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

B. 7 1/2 days
C. 10 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

\[

\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
V_w=72
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 72 litres

Q336. A team adds 15 metres to a project each day, but 5 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 150 metres?
A. 18 days
B. 19 days
C. 15 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 15 days

\]

\[
T=\frac{150}{15-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 15 days

Q337. If 30 people can use a food stock for 36 days, for how many days will the stock last for 40 people?
A. 22 days

\[

\]

\[
30\times 36=40\times D_2
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 27 days

Q338. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 15 days and B alone takes 30 days. How long does the job take?
A. 13 1/3 days
B. 18 1/2 days
C. 8 1/3 days
D. 12 1/3 days
Answer: 12 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
8(T-2)+4(T-3)=120
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=12\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 12 1/3 days

Q352. A pipe can fill a tank in 10 hours. Due to a leak, the tank is filled in 16 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty the full tank?
A. 32 2/3 hours
B. 33 1/3 hours
C. 29 2/3 hours
D. 26 2/3 hours
Answer: 26 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{16}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 hours

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{192}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

---

Q373. A job needs 24 days to finish. After 6 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 1/4
B. 3/1
C. 3/4
D. 4/3
Answer: 3/4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{24}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{18}{24}=3/4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{24-6}{24}=3/4
\]
Answer: 3/4

Q380. One worker can do a job in 6 days and another can do it in 30 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 18 days
B. 36 days
C. 5 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+1}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 30}{6+30}=5
\]
Answer: 5 days

\]

T=7
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 7 days

Q409. Without leakage, a tank fills in 15 hours. With leakage, it fills in 21 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?

Difficulty: Medium

\[

\]

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{21}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=52.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 52 1/2 hours

Q432. A alone finishes a job in 12 days, while B alone finishes it in 18 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 15 days
B. 12 days
C. 30 days
D. 7 1/5 days
Answer: 7 1/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,18)=36
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=36/12=3,\quad B=36/18=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{36}{3+2}=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 18}{12+18}=7.2
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-5}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q449. 8 equal workers begin a job. After 10 days, only 4 workers remain. By how many days is the work delayed?

\]

Q450. A takes 30 days to finish a work and B takes 18 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 24 days
B. 48 days
C. 11 1/4 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 11 1/4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,18)=90
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=90/30=3,\quad B=90/18=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{90}{3+5}=11.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 18}{30+18}=11.25
\]
Answer: 11 1/4 days

Q451. A repair team completes 15 metres per day and daily damage removes 3 metres. In how many days will 180 metres be completed?

Q452. A food stock lasts 30 days for 30 people. How many days will it last for 25 people?

Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]

\[

\]

\[
D_2=36
\]

D_2=\frac{30\times 30}{25}
\]
Answer: 36 days

Q453. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 18 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?
A. 6 3/5 days
B. 9 3/5 days
C. 14 3/5 days
D. 7 1/5 days
Answer: 9 3/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
15(T-2)+10(T-3)=180
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=9.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 3/5 days

Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{160}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 4 workers

---

Q484. A team takes 15 days for a job. After working for 6 days, what fraction of the job remains?
A. 2/5
B. 3/2
C. 3/5
D. 5/3
Answer: 3/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{15}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{9}{15}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-6}{15}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

Q488. A food stock would last 24 people for 25 days. After 6 days, more people join and the total becomes 30. Find the further duration in days?

Family: tw_food_population_change

\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}

Q489. Three workers take turns in the order A, B, C. They print 6, 6 and 3 pages on their turns. How many days are needed for 60 pages?
A. 14 2/5 days
B. 8 days
C. 14 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+6+3
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q490. A alone finishes a job in 12 days, while B alone finishes it in 6 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 18 days
B. 9 days
C. 4 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,6)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/12=1,\quad B=12/6=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{1+2}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 6}{12+6}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

---

Q493. There is enough food for 20 people for 36 days. What is its duration for 30 people?

B. 30 days

\]

Q494. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 24 days. How long does the job take?
A. 12 2/5 days
B. 8 1/3 days
C. 15 1/3 days
D. 10 1/3 days
Answer: 10 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
10(T-2)+5(T-3)=120
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=10\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 10 1/3 days

Q499. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 10 days and B alone needs 30 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?
